176. HENRY REDDING was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of February, 1 ass, price 30s., the property of William Rogers.
HENRY ROGERS. I am a farmer, and live at Iver. I lost this ass on the 25th of February, 1830; it used to stray about - I found it at Ealing about a fortnight after; it was a she ass, and a very remarkable one - I knew it to be mine; I had bought it seven weeks before; the person I bought it of knew her as well as I did.
JOSEPH HIGGINS. I apprehended the prisoner, and a man named Haynes, early in the morning of the 27th of February, 1830; they had this ass, and a great quantity of lead, which it was carrying - they both said the ass belonged to them, and they had come from Harrow; they were remanded three times, and then discharged - on the Friday following the prosecutor applied to me, and the prisoner was taken again; but he broke out of Uxbridge gaol on Sunday, the 20th of March, 1830; the other day he was in custody on another charge, and I stated this - Haynes was brought here, and pleaded guilty.
JOSEPH DICKINSON. I sold the ass to Mr. Rogers, and know it is the same.
Prisoner's Defence. I never owned it at all - Haynes owned it.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Transported for Seven Years.

On the 23rd of June I gave him ten sovereigns, to get silver for them, and he never returned - he was taken about a fortnight back, and said he had spent the money, and was very badly off.
FRANCIS FAGAN. I am an inspector of Police. I heard of the robbery, and have been looking for the prisoner - I took him on the 15th of November.
Prisoner. I was in great distress when it happened -I thought I would go somewhere, and earn the money; I will work it out by day or night.

182. DAVID REDDING was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of October, 1 earthen jar, value 2s.; 12 bottles, value 2s., and 2 gallons of wine, value 2l., the goods of Christopher Harrison and another, his masters.
WILLIAM WOLFE BONNY. I am in partnership with Mr. Christopher Harrison; we are wine merchant s, and live at Brompton; the prisoner was in our service; he worked on the premises, and carried out wine; we had missed wine, and suspected him. On the afternoon of the 9th of November, I discovered a bottle of gin - a bottle three-parts full, and one quite full of wine among the empty bottles; and as the bottle-house was under the direction of the prisoner, suspicion lighted on him - a lad gave me information, and I discovered that the prisoner had delivered a dozen of wine to Mr. Kirby, a surgeon, who produced a bottle of the wine, and came over with me to our house; we opened the bottle, my partner and I tasted it, and my opinion was that it came from No. 2 bin in our middle cellar - it was the oldest wine we had - I could swear to it; the bottle found among the empty bottles was our wine, but of a different sort - I cannot swear to the gin, as many other persons have gin from the same distiller; I was not aware of the loss of this earthen bottle, but it was found at the prisoner's.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANE. Q. Have you no more partners? A, No - Mr. Kirby said that the prisoner brought him the wine to place against an account; the prisoner was not in the habit of having wine of us; he had special permission once to have some; that was some months before.
WILLIAM KIRBY. I am a surgeon. When the prosecutor applied to me I gave him a bottle of wine, which was one of a dozen I had had from the prisoner in payment of a debt; I had had some of him some months before, by his master's permission - I think it was two dozen.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you live near the prosecutor's? A. Yes, they knew me very well - I have dealt with them.
MR. BONNY. I never authorized him to take any but once; on that occasion he said he owed Mr. Kirby a bill, and two dozen of wine would cover it, and we allowed him to take it; when the officer brought the prisoner round to our front door, I said he had been using me exceedingly ill, considering I used him well, and he never wanted any drink himself; he said he could not tell why I treated him so, and said he bought the dozen of wine of Frank Denny, at Thompson and Fearon's, but on producing Frank Denny at the office, he said, "That is not the man, it was John somebody that was with Thompson and Fearon, but I do not know where to find him;" on the second examination he said it was our wine, and he was willing to pay for it in any way if we would allow him.
Cross-examined. Q. I believe he had conducted himself well up to this time? A. Yes, we had him from Thompson and Fearon's, where he had been twelve months.
Prisoner's Defence. I have had the jar upwards of twelve months; Mr. Kirby sent for a dozen of wine, and I took it over - I meant to have entered it, but it slipped my memory.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-182]
183. JAMES REEVES was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of October, 2 lbs. 5 oz. of silk, value 3l., the goods of Thomas Walker.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS WALKER. I live in Fort-street, Spitalfields, and am a silk manufacturer; the prisoner served his time to me, and after that I allowed him loom-room; it was part of his business to weigh the silk for the other work people now and then. On the 25th of October I watched him, and when he came out of the warehouse I collaredhim, and found his pocket was full; I called a workman to assist me - we took him a little way, met an officer, and gave him into custody - some silk which he had been permitted to weigh that day was found in his pocket and in his hat - I had not permitted him to carry any away - he only begged for mercy.
WILFRED JOHNSON JAMES. I watched the prisoner going into the warehouse - I watched his pockets; they appeared to have nothing in them; I observed before he came out of the warehouse that his pockets were very bulky - he had been weighing silk for other workmen.
JOHN McGINNIS (Police-constable H 90). I took the prisoner, and found this silk in his hat and pocket - I found some silver and gold on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Months.

184. ELIZABETH RODWAY was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of November, 6 sovereigns, the monies of Francis Edward Bishop, in his dwelling-house; and GEORGE RODWAY and JOSEPH RODWAY were indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen.
FRANCIS EDWARD BISHOP. I am a herald painter and lodge in Gloucester-street, Clerkenwell. Elizabeth Rodway lived servant with me for about a week - she went home to sleep; my wife missed these sovereigns, and told me of it.
SOPHIA BISHOP. I am the prosecutor's wife. On the 2nd of November Elizabeth Rodway told me a person wanted me - I went to her, but she did not; I then suspected her, and on looking I missed six sovereigns out of a writing-desk, which I had left locked, and which had been unlocked, not broken open - the sovereigns had been wrapped in a piece of blue paper; the officer came and took her - she denied stealing them, but when my husband came home she acknowledged it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I believe you fell on your knees before her, and said, provided she would confess that she took the sovereigns, nothing should happen to her? A. I said if she produced the money - I did not say that God might strike me dead if she did not go harmless; she said that her brother George told her to steal the money from me, and she had given it to her brother Joseph - I did not show her some trinkets and gold, and ask her if she had ever seen so much.
EDWARD KENNARD (Police-constable G 95). I took the prisoner Elizabeth Rodway - she said she took the money and gave it to her younger brother, who gave it to the elder; I found on the elder the sovereigns, and this piece of blue paper.
Elizabeth Rodway 's Defence. Mrs. Bishop showed me the money, and asked if I should like so much; I said,"No, if it was not my own," and when I owned to it, she said she would not do any thing with me.
E. RODWAY - GUILTY. Aged 12.
Recommended to Mercy. - Judgment Respited .
G. RODWAY - NOT GUILTY.
J. RODWAY - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321129-184]
185. CHARLES MANNING was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of November, 1 watch, value 4l., the goods of William Horne.
HENRY MERRY. I live with Mr. William Horne, a watchmaker, in Ratcliff-highway. On the evening of the 20th of November, while my master went out, the prisoner came, and wished to look at a watch - we showed him some, which he did not like - at last he went out, and pointed to one in the window - he looked at that, and approved of it; he was to give 4l. for it, and to have a chain and seal into the bargain; I turned to get a chain and seal, and he ran out with the watch; I ran out, and two officers caught him with the watch.
GEORGE HARRISON (Police-constable K 64). I heard the cry of Stop thief! and saw the prisoner running - he had something white in his hand, and threw it away; I heard the smash of the glass, and picked up this watch.
JAMES WHITE (Police-constable K 285). I saw the prisoner come out of the shop and run - I overtook him as he was stooping, and trying to put the watch up a water-spout; I took him, and he threw it over my shoulder.
ANN HORNE. I am the prosecutor's wife. I was in the shop, and showed the prisoner the watches - what the boy has stated is correct.
Prisoner. I was out of employ.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Confined Nine Months.

186. DANIEL KALE was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of November, 1 half-crown, 2 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the monies of Nicholas Davison.
NICHOLAS DAVISON. I am a grocer, and live in Ratcliff-highway. On the 14th of November, I was at dinner, between twelve and one o'clock - the prisoner came in, and I saw him behind the counter; I went and asked what business he had there - he said he wanted some plums; I saw the till drawn out, and the money gone - I went after him and found the money on him.
JOHN GEORGE STATCHER (Police-serjeant H 7). I took the prisoner, and have the money.
Prisoner's Defence. My father sent me for this 5s., which I got; I called in at the prosecutor's shop, and knocked some time - he then came and asked what I wanted - I said, "Half a pound of plums;" he said he had none at my price.

187. ELIZABETH BATHURST was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of November, 1 pair of ear-rings, value 12s., and 1 glove, value 2d., the goods of Ann Balls, from her person.
ANN BALLS. I live in Nutfield-place, and am a dress-maker. I have known the prisoner three months; we lived together, but not in the same room. On the the 9th of November, I was walking in St. Martin's-lane with her, at half-past five o'clock in the evening; I had my gold ear-rings on - all on a sudden I received a blow on the side of my head, and fell down; when I recovered, I found myself in the arms of a Policeman - I did not miss my ear-rings for some time, but I missed my glove - I cannot tell who knocked me down, but the prisoner was there,and she said I had been knocked down by a female who had run down a passage - I did not see my ear-rings found; I had not been quarrelling with the prisoner - she saw gentlemen sometimes, and occasionally I do the same.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANE. Q. Is not the prisoner your landlady's daughter? A. Yes; we do not sleep together - we had gone out between twelve and one o'clock, to see the Lord Mayor's show - we had walked about all the time; she had not complained of being tired or hungry - she went to a pawnbroker's to pawn her bracelets, to buy some boots, to put on, but they would not take them in - we had been to a friends's to tea - we had a glass of gin each, but were not drunk.
JOSEPH OSTELL. I am street-keeper of St. Martin's-lane. I saw a crowd, and went to clear it away; I saw the prisoner pulling the prosecutrix about, and endeavouring to get her to go home; the prisoner said some woman had robbed her friend of her finger-rings, earrings, and an eye-glass; I asked her why she did not secure the party - she then said it was some man; I then began to have some suspicion of her, and said,"I shall take your friend to the station," and when we got there, I said to the prisoner, "I don't like this, I shall search you;" the prisoner then turned to a gentleman there, and said,"Will you fasten this bracelet on my wrist;" the gentleman did so, and while he did it, she dropped this glove, and its contents; I took it up, and found in it the ear-rings and finger-rings - she tried to put her foot on the glove, but I prevented her.(Property produced and sworn to).
Prisoner's Defence. I was with the prosecutrix, and a gentleman and lady were going through St. Martin's-court - the lady was jealous of the prosecutrix, and struck her; I turned back, and found her beating my frined - I asked her where her ear-rings were, and a gentleman took them up and gave them into her hand; she pulled off her glove to put them into her ears, but was too tipsy to do it - she was then taken to the watch-house, and there the glove and ear-rings were found, but I had not touched them.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321129-187]
188. JOHN BUCKMORE was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of October, 1 handkerchief, value 3s. 6d., the goods of Richard Attenborough and another; and that he had been before convicted of felony.
HENRY CHIDGEY. I was near the shop of Mr. Attenborough, in Crown-street, Finsbury, on the 25th of October; I saw the prisoner walk by, and take up the handkerchief, and look at it - I watched him; there was another with him; they went to an oil-shop, and then came back: the prisoner took the handkerchief, and I gave information.
JOHN BAWLEY. I live with the prosecutors. We had information, and I ran out; a man stopped the prisoner with the handkerchief in his hand - I took it from him; this is it - it has our mark on it.
GEORGE HANLEY. I am an officer. The prisoner was given into my custody.
RICHARD ATTENBOROUGH. This handkerchief belongs to me and my uncle.
WILLIAM ATTFIELD. I am an officer of Worship-street. I have a certificate (which I got at Mr. Clark's office) of the former conviction of the prisoner - I apprehended him, and know he is the man.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Transported for 14 Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-188]
189. WILLIAM ARMSTRONG was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of November, 1 hammer, value 2s., the goods of Richard Featherstone.
RICHARD FEATHERSTONE. This hammer is mine, and was lost in the Hay-market - my brother had lent it to a man named Perkins, who was working for Mr. Johnson.
JOHN FEATHERSTONE. On the 9th of November a young man gave me the hammer to take home to my brother; I put it behind me in the Hay-market, and it was gone.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANE. Q. Were there not a great many men working there? A. Yes.
WILLIAM ARMANDALE (Police-constable B 86) I found this hammer on the 23rd of November, in Market-street West, by the side of the prisoner.
NOT GUILTY.

190. CHARLES STEWART was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of November, 4 silver spoons, value 15s., the goods of William Howard.
FREDERICK HOWARD. I am the son of William Howard - he lives at Fulham. The prisoner was in his employ for fourteen days, to clean shoes and sweep the garden, two or three hours every morning; these spoons were missing on the 15th of November.
EDWARD MURVEN. I am shopman to Mr. James Stewart, a pawnbroker, at Putney. I have three spoons, which were pawned by the prisoner - I knew him before.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANE. Q. Is not your master the prisoner's grandfather? A. Yes - I asked the prisoner who they were for - he said Mrs. Brown, of Fulham, a housekeeper.
GEORGE GEVERS. I am in the service of a pawnbroker at Knightsbridge. This spoon was brought by the prisoner; I stopped him with it: he said he brought it for Mrs. Brown.(Property produced and sworn to.)

191. MARGARET ROACH was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of November, 6lbs. of beef, value 1s. 6d., the goods of George Anderson.
GEORGE ANDERSON. I am a butcher, and live in Brick-lane, Spitalfields. On the 17th of November, at half-past eight o'clock in the evening, I was in my shop, and saw the prisoner come and take the piece of beef off the corner of the stall; she put it under her shawl, and went to the next door - I went and took it from her; she pretended to be tipsy - she said it was not hers, and I might have it.
THOMAS ARNOLD (Police-constable H 127). I took the prisoner - she appeared to be very drunk, but when I got her to the watch-house she appeared quite sober.
Prisoner's Defence. I got a little drink, and do not know what I did.
GUILTY. Aged 41. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18321129-191]
192. BRIDGET BRODERIP was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of November, 40 half-crowns, 100 shillings, 80sixpences, and 1 bag, value 2d., the property of Michael Dwyer, in his dwelling-house.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
MARIA DWYER. I am the wife of Michael Dwyer - he keeps the Wellington public-house, Lucas-street, Commercial-road ; it is his dwelling-house. On Friday, the 9th of November, the prisoner came in about two o'clock in the afternoon; she asked for half a pint of beer, and asked me to warm it - I went into the parlour to the warming-pot; I had seen my till safe the instant before, when I gave the prisoner change - when I came back, I observed the till was partly out, but I did not suspect any thing; the prisoner finished her beer, and went away - when she was gone I examined the till, and missed a bag, containing from 12l. to 15l. in silver; I had seen it safe, and had my hand in the bag not a minute before she came in - I sent my pot-boy after her; he could not find her, but she came back in half an hour, and asked if I could get her a customer for a goose - I asked her for my money; she said,"What money?" I said, "The bag of money you took from my till;" she said had I lost a bag of money - I said,"Yes, and you have got it;" I asked her if any one had been at the bar while she was there - she said No; I said,"Then you have got it;" I sent for an officer, who took her - there was a 6d. in it, which I knew; the head was white, and it was black all round it.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANE. Q. She dealt in poultry, I believe? A. Yes - I had bought things of her; she expressed her astonishment when I charged her with it - she stripped herself, but nothing was found but 2s. 6d. in her pocket; I never told a servant girl that I thought one of Newton's dust-women had it - there was myself and the pot-boy at home, but the pot-boy was not at the bar.
SARAH AMOR. I am the wife of William Amor, a baker, in Lucas-street. On the 9th of November the pot-boy spoke to me, and in consequence of what he said, I went to my shop door, and saw the prisoner; I said to her, "Mistress, your are wanted at the Wellington;" she said, "Wanted at the Wellington?" I said, "Yes - the lad is in a hurry," but instead of going to the Wellington she went towards Cowdery's field.
MARY MATTHEWS. I live in Vinegar-lane. On the 9th of November I saw the prisoner going towards Cowdery's field, between one and two o'clock.
SAMUEL CHESTER. I am a sail-maker, and live in Union-terrace. On Saturday morning, the 10th of November, I was going to work across Cowdery's field - I saw a shilling; I looked further, and found 3l. 14s. 6d. in silver - I gave it to my master, all but 4s.
PETER DOYLE. I am Chester's master. He gave me the money to take care of, and said he found it in Cowdery's field.
JAMES KNIGHT. I am a waterman. I went into Cowdery's field, and found a shilling; Daniel Smith told me to go - I saw him have eight half-crowns, two shillings, and some sixpences.
WILLIAM OSBORNE (Police-constable K 66). I was called to the Wellington, and the prisoner was given in charge - she said she was innocent; but in going to the watch-house she seemed flurried, and asked for my hand; I gave it her, and she put half a crown in it, told me to do what I could for her, and she would make it all right.
MRS. DWYER. This sixpence was among the money that was lost.
Cross-examined. Q. Dirty sixpences are not very uncommon? A. I have had it three or four months - it has been repeatedly offered in change, and refused.
Prisoner's Defence. I went for change for a shilling, and asked her to warm me half a pint of beer, which she did - I then came out with the two sixpences she had given me; I then went to a lady's, and received half a crown - I went across Cowdery's field in my way home, but did not go where the money was found - I then returned to the witness, to offer her a goose I had to sell, and she said the boy from the Wellington was looking for me; I went there- I gave the half-crown to the officer to get some beer for us to drink.
GUILTY. Aged 45. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18321129-192]
193. MARY REDDINGTON was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of November, 4 half-crowns, and 5s. in copper monies, the monies of Timothy Tuomey, from the person of Bridget, his wife.
BRIDGET TUOMEY. I am the wife of Timothy Tuomey, a labourer, who lives in Ratcliff-highway. On the 12th of November, between nine and ten o'clock, I was in Bluegate-fields - I saw the prisoner, whom I knew; she asked me to come home, as her husband was ill-using her - I went into the passage of her house, and she knocked me down, with my child in my arms; she took my money out of my pocket - it was tied up in a small silk handkerchief; there were four half-crowns and 5s. in copper - she struck me, and threw me out of the door; a man threw mud over me, took my cap, and made pieces of it - I went home, told my husband, and then gave the prisoner in charge; I never got my money again.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Did you not consider the prisoner a friend? A. Yes; I have not been intimate with her husband - I have quarrelled with her about a little money; we did not quarrel in going to her house - my child fell out of my arms when she knocked me down; my husband has 1l. a week - I had been saving up the money to bury a child in the hospital.
TIMOTHY TUOMEY. I am the husband of this witness. The prisoner came and said her husband was kicking up a row with her; my wife went with her, and returned with her face black and bloody, and her gown torn and full of dirt - she said she had been robbed of 15s.; I saw her change a sovereign in the morning, and she paid some to the woman at the next door, who used to give her bread.
HENRY LEE (Police-constable K 262). The witness came to me in a very bad state; I took the prisoner in her room.
Cross-examined. Q. Was there any confusion or quarrel? A. No; I found nothing on the prisoner, but in a cap on the mantel-piece I found a half-crown, two penny-pieces, and one halfpenny.
Prisoner's Defence. She followed me, and attempted to strike me in my own house; I struck her, and we fought, but I never saw her money. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321129-193]
194. THOMAS NORMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of November, 1 watch, value 15s., and 1 gold chain, value 1l., the goods of John Warder, and 2 salt holders, value 5s., the goods of Catherine Warder .
CATHERINE WARDER. I am a widow; I keep a chinaand glass-shop, in Great Marylebone-street. The prisoner came to my shop between eleven and twelve o'clock on the 27th of November - he asked for a pitcher; I went down stairs to get it: when I brought it up, he said I might as well send it to Mr. Oliver's, No. 10, Devonshire-place, whom we supply with things - I afterwards found Mr. Oliver did not want it; my son returned in half an hour after the prisoner was gone, and missed his watch, chain, and the salts; the prisoner could easily have taken the watch - he stood just opposite to it; he did not say he was a servant of Mr. Oliver's.
THOMAS HUGHES. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Hampstead-road. The prisoner pawned the watch with me on the 27th of November, and the prosecutor redeemed it; the prisoner then came to redeem it, and I stopped him - he ran off, and the officer took him.
JOHN MICHAEL JOLLY (Police-constable T 164). I took the prisoner, and found on him two duplicates; one of an umbrella, pawned for 2s., and the other a pair of salts.
THOMAS PEWTNER. I am a pawnbroker, I have the salts, which were pawned by the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I did it through distress.
GUILTY. Aged 36.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Months.

195. FELIX PERRY was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of November, 1 cap, value 8s., the goods of Joseph Spencer, from the person of John Spencer .
JOSEPH SPENCER. The prisoner was brought to me by a neighbour, and I was informed he had stolen a cap from my son John's head - this is the cap; I know it is my son's- he had been sent to school with it, and as he came back this happened; he is six years old.
ROBERT MANN. I was coming along Myrtle-street, and heard the child scream; the prisoner ran towards me: I caught him - he said, "Pray, let me go," and the cap dropped from him - he said, "Allow me to go back to the child, and I will give it him."
JOHN BRAND (Police-constable N 199). I have the cap, and took the prisoner.
GUILTY of stealing only. Aged 14.

196. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Harvey Hill, from his person.
HARVEY HILL. On the 26th of November I was walking down the Commercial-road; when I got through the Cannon-street-road turnpike, I observed the prisoner behind me; I missed my handkerchief, and saw him crossing the road - a young man stopped him for me, and the officer took him; he asked him if he had my handkerchief - he said he had not, but he was searched at the watch-house, and it was found in his possession; this is it.
GEORGE CHAMBERS (Police-constable K 99). My attention was called to the prisoner; he was asked if he had the prosecutor's handkerchief - he denied it, and said he had not; he was willing to be searched, and to go to the station - I found this handkerchief in the flap of his trousers.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.

197. ELIZABETH DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of November, 14 shillings, the monies of William Martin, her master.
WILLIAM MARTIN. I keep the Blue Posts, in King-street, Golden-square . The prisoner was in my employ for eight or nine months; I gave her money at different times to purchase articles, but not any specific amount.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321129-197]
198. ANTHONY ARNOLD was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of August, 1 wooden plank, value 17s., the goods of William Evans; and that he had been before convicted of felony.
WILLIAM EVANS. I live in Shepherdess-walk, Hoxton. On the 28th of August I missed an ash plank from Mr. Sturts' wharf, on the bank of the Regent's-canal - I had seen it safe on the Saturday, and missed it on Tuesday morning; I found it the same day at Upstill's, a wheeler, in the Canal-road - I know it by my mark on it; it is worth 17s. - the prisoner had no right to sell any timber for me; I do not know that I ever saw him.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not take money of the man for the plank? A. No, he did not look it out; he asked the price of it, and said he would come on the Monday and see if he could deal with me for it.
CHARLES UPSTILL. I am a wheelwright, and live in the Canal-road. On the 24th of August I went to Mr. Evans' wharf, and asked the price of the plank - he asked 17s.; on the 27th I went there again - I saw the prisoner and another man; the prisoner asked what I wanted - I said I had looked at the plank, and asked if the governor was in the way; he said No, but he could do as well - I said it was all right, and I would send for a truck - he said there was no occasion for it; he would bring it home for half a gallon of beer - he brought it, and I saw my wife pay him for it.
Prisoner. Q. You asked if you had not given too much for the plank? A. No, you asked what I wanted, and I told you what the price was - I said it was 1s. a foot; I did not ask if there was a truck in the yard - I proposed to go for one, and then you said you and your mate would take it home; I suppose it was five or six o'clock- I did not see you at the Bricklayer's Arms; it was a very wet day - you received the money.
JAMES GLIBBERY (Police-constable N 248). On the 28th of August I went with the prosecutor to Upstill's; I found the plank there.
THOMAS HOBBS KING (Police-constable N 248). I apprehended the prisoner last month at Pentonville - I told him it was for stealing an ash plank of Mr. Evans; he said he had seen Mr. Evans, and settled with him - I took him to the station, and then went to Mr. Evans', who said he had not seen him.
THOMAS COPE. I know the prisoner. On the 8th of February, 1829, I apprehended him for stealing from Messrs. Kings, carpenters, at Islington, two deal planks - he was found guilty, and find 1s.; I have the certificate of his former conviction, which I got from Mr. Clark's office.
Prisoner. It must be my partner who sold it; I never received the money - when he got it outside the yard, I thought I would help him take it home.
MR. EVANS. I never authorized him to receive the money, and he never paid it to me; my gates were locked, and the plank was drawn under them.
JAMES GLIBBERY. The prisoner and his mate were brought to the station the same night, drunk, and they had each of them some money.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that his fellow-workman had told him he was to take the plank to the witness, and that the money was paid to his mate.
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-198]

200. JOHN BRYANT was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of November, 1 coat, value 20s., the goods of Richard Shearing.
RICHARD SHEARING. I lost the coat, but I know nothing of the means by which it was taken - the officer is not here.

201. SAMUEL BUSHELL was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 1 coral-drop, value 7s., the goods of John Garritt.
JOHN GARRITT. I live in Ray-street, Clerkenwell, and am a lapidary - the prisoner was my servant. On the 20th of November I had a large sized coral-drop in a box in my shop; I discharged the prisoner, and then missed it; this is it.
Prisoner. Q. Are you not in the habit of looking your coral over almost every day? A. Just as my customers may require it; I know this piece by a hole, about half an inch from the top of it, where a branch has been broken off; there are frequently holes, but not like this; I know it from its being an odd drop - I had tried to match it, and could not; it is the largest piece that was left in the box; it weighs about 4 dwts. 16 grains.
WILLIAM WHITELOCK. I am a working-jeweller, and live in Northampton-street. I bought this coral-drop of the prisoner for 3s. 6d.; I asked him if he worked for Mr. Garritt; he said No - (he had before brought me a pair of drops;) I sent to Mr. Garritt to know if he worked for him, and he said he did; he then brought me another pair of drops, and I sent to ask Mr. Garritt if he had sold him any drops - he said he had sold him three pairs; I bought three pairs, and this odd one of the prisoner; on the Monday Mr. Garritt called, and I told him I had bought this drop of the prisoner - he said it was his, and one he had been looking for.
ANDREW LLOYD. I am an officer of Hatton-garden. I apprehended the prisoner; he wanted to know what I charged him with - I said, "With taking a coral-drop;" he said he had sold three pairs to Mr. Whitelock, but he knew nothing about the odd one.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he was in the habit of purchasing goods of the prosecutor, and selling them on his own account.
JOHN GARRITT. I believe the prisoner had one pair of me some months ago, and three pairs lately, but I never sold him this; I am quite positive it is mine.
GUILTY. Aged 48.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18321129-201]
202. EDWARD HOWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of October, 13 knives and 14 forks, value 10s.; 1 bottle of ale, value 1s.; 1 sponge, value 6d.; 7 pieces of soap, value 2s.; 2 wine-bottles, value 6d.; and 1 wine-glass, value 6d., the goods of James Smith and another, his masters; and JAMES HOWELL was indicted for feloniously receiving part of the said goods, well knowing them to have been stolen.
JOHN FREDERICK SMITH. I am in partnership with my brother James - we keep the Angel Inn, Pentonville - Edward Howell was our porter for about two years. On the 30th of October, in consequence of suspicion, I watched with my brother - I heard a noise, about six o'clock in the morning, of breaking in at the area; I went to the cellar, and head some one removing the bottles -I stood a short time, and saw Edward Howell coming from the cellar, he was in into the one I was in; I knew he must see me, as he had a light - I collared him - he said, "Oh Lord! what have I done," and begged for mercy - a bottle of ale had been removed to the grating of the cellar, and a piece of soap - we had a quantity of soap in that cellar, the whole of which had been taken away but seven cakes; I saw a box searched in Edward Howell 's bed-room, but the under waiter sleeps with him - James Howell had lived in my service a year before; I went to his lodgings, and found a mahogany case, containing white-handled and black-handled knives and forks, and a number of bottles, two of which I had marked five weeks before in my cellar, and I did not allow them to be sold - he said he had bought the knives and forks, and would not give the officer any answer about the bottles - he afterwards said Mr. Waterhouse gave him part of them: here are my marks on them.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You put the marks on the bottles five weeks before? A. Yes, after James Howell had left me - there was nothing on them to let him know they were mine; I had lost a great many - I marked about two hundred bottles: James Howell was in bed - he gave us every facility to search his place.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANE. Q. Did you pay Edward Howell 's wages? A. No, he had no wages, only what gentlemen gave him.
COURT. Q. What reason had you to know that Edward Howell had been to the area where the cake of soap and bottle of ale were? A. The grating had been taken up.
WILLIAM SMITH. I am the prosecutor's brother, and live in White Lion-street. I saw Edward Howell taken - he begged for mercy; some soap was found in a box in the room in which he slept.
JOHN MATTHEWS. I am a waiter at the Angel. I have seen the knives and forks produced, and know them to be the prosecutors', from their passing through my hands every day for several years; Edward Howell had to clean them ever since he has been there, which is about eighteen months; James has occasionally assisted his brother in a morning.
HENRY ALLEN (Police-constable N 250). I found EdwardHowell in the cellar - he said, "Oh, Mr. Smith, pray have mercy on me;" I saw the bottle of ale and cake of soap at the window - I asked him at the watch-house for the key of his box; he said I should find it in his jacket pocket -I opened the box, and this other soap was found in it.
JAMES COLLINS (Police-constable N 94). I saw the key found, the soap taken from the box, and a wine-glass which was marked "Angel Inn:" I found on Edward Howell at the station, another key, which opened the cellar door - I went to James Howell's, and found these articles; he said he bought the knives and forks in Goswell-road, and afterwards he said at Mr. Essex's, opposite the church at Islington.
E. HOWELL - GUILTY. Aged 36.
Transported for Seven Years.
J. HOWELL - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321129-202]
203. EDWARD RICHARD BURCH was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of October, 1 brooch, value 10s., the goods of Richard Clay.
ELIZABETH LEATHERDALE. I am the wife of John Leatherdale. The prisoner was in my husband's employ; I placed a brooch in my back room, where the prisoner slept, and a gold pin, on the 18th of October - I missed them the next morning; I found the pin in the prisoner's bed; the brooch I could not find - the prisoner said he had not seen it; it belonged to Richard Clay - it had been repaired by a jeweller, and sent to me.
JOHN EASTERBROOK (Police-serjeant K 25). I saw the prisoner on the night of the 25th of October - I watched him to a house in Turner-street, Ratcliff-highway; I went and searched him - I found on him the duplicate of the brooch; I asked where he got it - he said he found it a week before, but in going to the station he said he found it in some saw-dust at his master's; when he got to the station he told the inspector he found it in the cellar - he said he did not return because his master made so much bother.
THOMAS EELY. I am shopman to a pawnbroker. I have a brooch, pawned on the 24th of October, by the prisoner; he was in a fustain frock - this is the duplicate I gave him.
Prisoner. Q. You said you could not swear to me? A. Yes, but I have collected my thoughts, and can swear to you now.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. They take in several lodgers, and I used to get duplicates from the sailors, by giving them gin for them; Mrs. Leatherdale said she did not know whether she left it on the table or on my bed - they might have fallen on the floor; I found it in some saw-dust.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18321129-203]
OLD COURT. WEDNESDAY, DECEMBER 5TH.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
204. ARTHUR OWEN was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of November, 3 pairs of shoes, value 7s., the goods of Walter Pitman.
GEORGE ROWLAND.I am in the service of Walter Pitman, a shoemaker. About two o'clock in the afternoon of the 9th of November, I saw the prisoner and another about the shop - I saw the prisoner take three pairs of shoes off the nail at the door, and hand them to his companion, who made off with them - I followed; his companion escaped, but I took the prisoner, without losing sight of him; he dropped a ticket, which had been attached to the shoes - I am positive of him.
THOMAS BOLTWOOD. I received him in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in Chiswell-street - two women called out that a man had run away with the shoes; I saw a man running from the shop with them, but lost sight of him; I turned back, and met the prosecutor - the Policeman actually saw the thief run by him.
GEORGE ROWLAND. He turned round as if to strike me, and the ticket fell from his coat.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-204]
205. HENRY PATTERSON was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of November, 1 sovereign, the money of John Gee.
JOHN GEE. I am a servant to Mr. North, of High-street, Shoreditch. This sovereign was in a box in my bed-room; I missed three within about a fortnight - the prisoner slept in the same bed with me; I spoke to Mr. North, my master, about it, and he marked four sovereigns: on the 23rd of November, in the evening; I got up first in the morning, and went into the shop - I returned afterwards, and missed one sovereign; I always missed one at a time - I named it to my employer; he got an officer, and the sovereign was found in the prisoner's box, with the mark on it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. How long was he in your master's employ? A. About two months; I never quarrelled with the prisoner - his principles were rather different to mine; I corrected him when he used to swear - we never went to blows; his box was kept in the same room as mine - I left the key in my box, as I always did, and after the money was marked I wished to ascertain how it went; I generally went to bed after the prisoner - I do not know where the key of his box was kept.
WILLIAM GLOVE. I am an officer. I was sent for - I searched the prisoner's box in the bed-room, and found a sovereign; I asked him if he had any money - he said Yes, he had a sovereign; I asked where he kept it - he pointed to a part of the box, and said, "Here," and gave it me.
Cross-examined. Q. He at once said he had it? A. Yes; I searched the box, and could not find it - he then pointed to a drawer in his box, and showed it to me.
JOHN GEE. This is the sovereign which my master marked.
ALEXANDER NORTH. In consequence of what Gee told me I marked four sovereigns; I did not see Gee put them into his box - I know this to be one of the sovereigns I marked.
Cross-examined. Q. You were not present at any part of the transaction? A. I went up stairs with the Policeman; the prisoner took the key out of his pocket, and I think opened the box himself - he took out two trays; the Policeman began to search the bottom of the box - the prisoner said, "I have only one sovereign," and produced it; I believe it is his first offence.
JURY. Q. Did they quarrel together? A. No, except on religious questions; the prosecutor appears very religious, and the prisoner quite different.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-205]
206. WILLIAM SMITH and WILLIAM JENKINS were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of November, 4 live tame pigeons, price 3s. the property of William Deeley.
CHARLES JONES. I deal in birds and rabbits, and live in Pancras-place, Pancras. On the 26th of November, at a quarter before six o'clock in the evening, the prisoner Smith and another boy brought me two pigeons, and asked a shilling for them; and seeing me not anxious to take them they said they were dissolving partnership, and had more to sell - I then bought them for 2s., and in a few minutes a Policeman brought the two prisoners in: I said Smith was one of them that had sold me the pigeons - Deeley selected the two I had bought.
CHARLES STEWART. I am an officer. I stopped the prisoners with each a pigeon; Jenkins' brother was with them - I asked where they got them; Jenkins said he had bought them at Fletcher's, a pigeon-fancier - I asked them where they got the money to buy them; he said he had held a horse for a gentleman - I was taking them to Fletcher's, and Smith said, "We did not buy them there, we bought them here," pointing to Jones' - I took them there, and from what he said, took them to the station.
WILLIAM DEELEY. I deal in pigeons - I live in Brill-row, Somers-town; I do not know the prisoners; I lost these pigeons on Tuesday the 27th of November, out of my shop window - a lath had been broken out of my shop window, and the pigeons taken - I know these to be them; I saw them before the Magistrate.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 13.
JENKINS - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Whipped and Discharded.
[trialAccount t18321129-206]
207. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 2 carriage lamps, value 10s., the goods of Sarah Clarke.
CHARLES JENNINGS. I am a Policeman. On the 26th of November, about eight o'clock, I saw the prisoner and two others, at the corner of Judd-street and Speldhurst-street, Brunswick-square; seeing me they immediately separated - I saw the prisoner had something under his arm; I followed him to Coram-street; he turned into a dark corner, and threw something down; I immediately laid hold of him, and asked what he had there - he said nothing; I told him to pick up what he had put down; he said I might be d - d; I gave him to another officer -I found two carriage lamps there, which I found belonged to the prosecutrix.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see any thing under my arm? A. I did.
JOHN HERRING. I drive a fly for Sarah Clarke, who is a stable-keeper; I came in about seven o'clock; a carriage stood in front of the gateway in Speldhurst-street; the lamps were all secure then; I did not miss them till the officer brought them to me.
Prisoner's Defence. I stopped to speak to a young man; I left him, and the Policeman followed me and said, "What have you got?" I said, "I have nothing" - he said, "Pick up what you have there" - I said, "I don't know that any thing is there."
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-207]
208. ANN TURNER was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of November, 1 shawl, value 7s., the goods of George Ravenors.
HENRY NEWSTEAD. I am a shopman to George Raveoor, a pawnbroker, in Tothill-street, Westminster. On the 30th of November this shawl hung about a foot within the doorway - I was behind the counter, and saw it drop; I went to pick it up, and found it was gone; I went out, and saw the prisoner turn round the end of the window, down a passage; I ran after her, and asked what she had wrapped up in a piece of cotton, which she had; she said,"What she had was her own;" I took it from her, and it turned out to be a shawl - it had been pulled down from the door, having been fastened with a string.(Property produced and sworn to).
Prisoner's Defence. A woman had the shawl in her hand, and said, "I have been to fetch this out of pawn; hold it for me till I come out again, I am going to fetch some more;" she went in once more, and walked on before me; I was following to give it her; my husband was a quarter-master in the 1st regiment of the Guards; he is dead.
GUILTY. Aged 45. Confined for Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18321129-208]
Before Mr. Recorder.
209. RICHARD VANDOME was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of November, 1 coat, value 3l., the goods of George Palmer.
GEORGE PALMER. I keep a beer-shop in Brick-lane. On Sunday, the 18th of November, about ten minutes after nine o'clock at night, I put my coat behind the counter on a barrel of ale, folded up - I have seen the prisoner at the shop several times; he was sitting on the opposite side of the counter at this time - I missed it in about ten minutes, he was then gone; somebody said he had seen the prisoner go out with it - I took a person who had sat by his side to the station-house, and in consequence of what he said I sent the Policeman to No. 24, John-street, Brick-lane - he found the coat, and brought it to the station.
JOHN POND. I was at the beer-shop, and saw the prisoner there; and a few minutes after nine o'clock I saw him walk out with the coat under his arm - it struck me afterwards that it did not belong to him, and I gave information, as I missed it from the barrel; he was taken in an hour.
JAMES MILTON. I am a cabinet-maker, and live in Lamb's-fields. I went to Palmer's house for a pot of porter - I saw the prisoner come out of the beer-shop with the coat over his arm; I went in, and heard Palmer had lost one, and told him what I had seen - I am certain of him.
JAMES JOHN CONNELLY. I am a Policeman. I was at the station-house - a man was brought there on suspicion; I went and found the prisoner at No. 28, St. John-street - I knocked at the door and asked if Buck lived there, which name was given me at the station-house; I went up to the front room first floor, and found the prisoner there - I told him I took him for stealing the coat from the beer-shop, and found it laying under his other clothes, in a chair at the foot of the bed; Palmer claimed it - the prisoner said afterwards, that had he not been drunk he should not have taken it.
Prisoner's Defence. Pond said after he missed the coat that I was in the place five minutes with it, and about ayard from the prosecutor, but he never told him - a person there got into conversation about business, and then asked me to take the coat for him to John-street, and to tell his wife he should be home in an hour; when I got into the air the liquor took effect on me, and I thought I would take it home in the morning.
GUILTY. Aged 26. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-209]
210. THOMAS WILLICOMBE was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of November, 6 loaves of bread, value 2s. 4d., and 1 piece of green baize, value 1s. 2d., the goods of Abraham Phillips Bromwich, his master.
ABRAHAM PHILLIPS BROMWICH. I was a baker. On the 14th of November I lived in Tottenham-court-road, the prisoner had been in my employ about six months - he used to go out with bread in the day time; I cannot swear that I missed any loaves - the prisoner worked in the night time.
EDMUND DAVIS. I am a Policeman. On the 14th of November, about half-past two o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoner going up Tottenham-court-road, about two hundred yards from Mr. Bromwich's, with something under his arm - I crossed over to him, stopped him, and asked what he had got, and where he got it from; he said he got it from Mr. Bromwich's, and he was going to take it to a biscuit-baker - I asked where the biscuit-baker lived - he pointed to a mews; I asked him to show me where it was; I accompanied him there, and found a biscuit-baker there named Richard Shackelford - I took him back to his master's; he had these six loaves in a green baize - he offered me a crown to let him go; I showed his master the bread and cloth, he said it was his property - it was not new bread; he said he could swear to the green baize.
ABRAHAM PHILLIPS BROMWICH. Shackelford had been a servant of mine - I never authorized the prisoner to take this bread to him; I have not a doubt that the bread is mine, and the green baize I can swear to - it had been a table-cloth, and was used for a roll-cloth; the prisoner lived in my house, but Shackelford lived at home.
JAMES COLLINS. I am a Policeman. I was with Davis; his account is correct - I did not hear the prisoner offer a crown; I found two keys on the prisoner, and on searching his waistcoat-pocket found a key which belonged to the prosecutor's till.
ABRAHAM PHILLIPS BROMWICH. This is the key of my till, which I lost a fortnight before - the other key belongs to a closet in his room, which he had the use of.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-210]
211. JAMES WALKER was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 1 wheelbarrow, value 8s., the goods of William Burton.
WILLIAM BURTON. On the 26th of November I left my barrow safe on the area-railing, in front of my door, about six o'clock in the evening - I went out; I returned at ten o'clock, and it was gone; I saw it about half-past ten o'clock that night at Mr. Brightman's, China-street, St. Pancras, and was quite positive of it.
BENJAMIN BEIGHTMAN. I keep a coffee-shop in China-street, St. Pancras. About a quarter-past eight o'clock the prisoner came to my shop - I knew him by sight before - he pleaded distress, saying he had come from Highgate with a barrow, and had brought a man with him to wheel it for him, with a box, and he asked me to lend him a few shillings - I said I could not afford it, but he pleaded distress, and a man was with him, who he said he had got to wheel it, as he was a cripple, and he wanted to pay him; I at last lent him 2s. on the barrow, and he promised to pay me next day at twelve o'clock - I saw Mr. Burton about ten, and he claimed it.
JOSEPH WOOD. I do porter's work. I was in the prosecutor's house - the prisoner came there about half-half-past six o'clock in the evening - the house was in Little Drummond-street, Somers'-town; he asked the price of 1 cwt. of potatoes - he came in and said he could get them in Chapel-street for 3s. 6d; the prosecutrix said her husband was out, and he had better come in the morning - he said, "Will you come and have a glass of gin?" but I refused, and about ten minutes after he was gone the barrow was missing; I afterwards saw it at the office, and knew it.
EDMUND CANDY. I am a Policeman. I had information of this, and took the prisoner in Regent's-park; he said he met a man at Battle-bridge, with the barrow, who asked him if he knew where he could sell it.
Prisoner's Defence. A person asked if I knew who would buy it - I said No; I was in liquor - I went with him, and offered it for sale.
BENJAMIN BRIGHTMAN. He appeared to have had beer, but was not intoxicated.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18321129-211]
212. CHARLES WOOD was indicted for embezzlement.
BENJAMIN BENWELL. I live at No. 74, Gower-place, Euston-square, and am a baker. The prisoner was about six months in my service - he carried out bread, and received money for it; Maria Cale, Harriet Taylor, and Susan Connell were customers of mine - he never paid me any of the money in question from them; he should pay it to me on the day he received it - we settled accounts every evening.
MARIA CALE. I live at No. 4, Milton-street, Euston-square, as servant to Mr. Brown, who was a customer of Mr. Benwell's. The prisoner brought a bill on the 27th of October, for 7s. 10d., and I paid him - he was charged with this offence in about a week.
HARRIET TAYLOR. I live at No. 13, Milton-street, Euston-square. I deal with Mr. Brown - the prisoner brought me a bill on Saturday, the 27th of October, for 2s. 5 1/2d.; I paid him on the Monday or Tuesday following.
SUSAN CONNELL. I live at No. 1, Phillips-gardens, St. Pancras - I keep the house. The last payment I made to the prisoner was on the 13th of November, I paid him 3s. 2d. - he brought the bill; I gave him 3s., as he could not give me change for 4s., and he signed the bill.
MR. BENWELL. He never accounted to me for any of these sums - here is his account.
DAVID LAING. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner into custody in Oxford-street, on the 19th of November; I asked where he worked - he said with theprosecutor; I said I was looking for him, and took him to the prosecutor, who charged him with this - he said he had embezzled several sums of money from his master.
MR. BENWELL. He had run away in the morning as the Policeman took him in the evening; he was jobbing for a friend, who recommended him to me.
GUILTY. Aged 20. Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-212]
213. EDWARD WEBB was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of November, 3 shillings, 2 sixpences, and 24 halfpence, the monies of Maria Fermage.
MARIA FERMAGE. I am single, and live in Flight-street, Portland-town - the prisoner lives in the same street as I do; I keep a chandler's-shop - this money was in the till. On the 5th of November I went out, and left my sister in charge of the shop - she is very weak in her intellects - when I returned home she showed me the place the till was kept in; the till, money, and all were gone - I had left nine shillings and some halfpence and pence in it; the prisoner has parents, I believe.
JAMES WATT. I am a Policeman. I apprehended the prisoner on the 10th of November; I told him he was charged with robbing Miss Fermage's till - he denied it.
JAMES OLIFF. I live in High-street, nearly opposite Miss Fermage's - I have seen the prisoner before. On the 5th of November I saw him with a till in Henry-street, between five and six o'clock in the evening; I did not speak to him - I did not see where he went to - I heard money rattle in the till.
JAMES WHYBRID. I am a gardener, and live in Frederick-street, Portland Nursery-gardens. I found the till in my garden on the 9th of November - there was no money in it; I gave it to a Policeman named Dody, who gave it to the Police-serjeant; Miss Fermage claimed it.
FANNY BAITS. Between five and seven o'clock on the 5th of November, I saw the prisoner run by me with the till; I am quite sure he is the lad - I have seen him playing at marbles before; his father and mother have turned him out of doors.
MISS FERMAGE. I am sure this is my till - I have had it seven years.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not do it; I was not near the place.
GUILTY. - Aged 13. - Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18321129-213]
214. ANN WALKER was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of November, 1 silver spoon, value 2s.; 2 shirts, value 15s.; 1 book, value 2s.; 1 apron, value 4d., and 1 handkerchief, value 2d., the goods of Jane Wakefield.
JANE WAKEFIELD. I am a widow. I lost these articles - I missed the spoons on the 28th of August, and on the 16th of November two shawls - the prisoner lived in my house; I have known her thirteen years; she had access to every thing.
JOSEPH CHIDWICK. I am a Policeman. I was called in on the 19th of November; the prosecutrix said she had lost a number of things; among others a silver spoon, two shawls, a book, and an apron - the prisoner denied the charge - I searched her, and found a number of tickets; she then told me where the shawls were pawned - I went there, and found one; the other was produced by a pawnbroker, who had the silver spoon; I found the book and the apron.
WILLIAM MINDOCK. I am a servant to Mr. Clunes, of Liquor-pond-street, a pawnbroker. I have a sheet, pawned on the 10th of August, by the prisoner, for 4s., in the name of Ann Williams, Rosamond-street.
RICHARD CHARLES. I am apprentice to Mr. Short, of St. John-street. I have a book, apron, and handkerchief, pawned at different times; the apron and book were pawned on the 6th of October, with two spoons, by the prisoner - I am quite sure she is the person; the book was pawned in the name of Rowland.
GEORGE CLARKSON. I am apprentice to Mr. Drew, of Clark's-place, Islington. I have a shawl, pawned by the prisoner, on the 17th of August, and a tea-spoon on the 18th, in the name of Mary Clarke.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner handed in a petition for a lenient sentence.
GUILTY. Aged 65. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-214]
215. JAMES WARD was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of November, 1 mattress, value 18s., the goods of Daniel Biass.
DANIEL CLEARY. I am in the employ of Daniel Biass, of Oxford-street, an upholsterer. On the 12th of November I saw this mattress safe, in the rack at the door, about ten minutes before the Policeman brought the prisoner into the shop with it.
GEORGE ANTHONY. I am a writer and grainer. On Monday night, the 12th of November, I saw the prisoner in company with another young man, and after some time he walked by this shop, turned back, took the mattress off the rack, put it on his shoulder, and crossed the road; the Policeman stopped him - his companion made off: I should know him again.
RICHARD PEARCE. I am a Policeman. I stopped the prisoner fifty or sixty yards from the shop - the other person ran away; I took the prisoner back, with the mattress.
Prisoner. He had the other man in custody at the time. Witness. No, the other ran away.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-215]
216. SARAH WATTS was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of November, 3 pairs of trousers, value 6s.; 1 shawl, value 6d., and 2 yards of linen-cloth, value 1s., the goods of Frances Kelly.
FRANCES KELLY. I am a widow. On the 6th of November I lodged in Wilks-street, Spitalfields; the prisoner worked at slop-work for me - she came to my room for work; I gave her these things to take to Mr. Clark, in Union-row, Tower-hill, who they belonged to, and to bring me the money - it was slop-work; I have never seen them since; I had known her about two years.
GEORGE MARCHANT. I am servant to Mr. Clark. These trousers were given to the prosecutrix to be made up; they have never been brought home - it was my duty to enter the goods that came in, and I am sure they never were returned.
JAMES WAYLING. I went to Spitalfields workhouse, and took the prisoner - I asked her what had become of the trousers, the wrapper, and shawl; she said she met a woman, who gave her something to drink, and she was knocked down, and lost them.
GUILTY. Aged 49. - Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-216]
217. THOMAS WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 2 wheels, value 6s., the goods of Samuel Drawater.
SAMUEL DRAWATER. I hawk earthernware about in a waggon. The two wheels of my waggon hung in my back yard, in Rose-lane, Spitalfields; I saw them safe on Wednesday, and on Monday they were taken - I found the prisoner in custody with them; he was a stranger.
SAMUEL GREEN. I am a Policeman I saw the prisoner on Monday morning, the 26th of November, about half-past nine o'clock, at the top of White's-row, Spitalfields, about a hundred yards from the prosecutor's; he had one wheel in each hand - he lifted up his head, saw me, and turned back; I went round Butler-street, and then found the wheels standing against the wall - I saw him a few yards off, and took him; he said he had bought them that morning, for 1s. 6d., at the back of the Cross Keys, of a man he did not know.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. About half-past seven o'clock I saw a man in Rose-lane with the wheels - he offered them for sale; I said I had but 18d. - he followed me, and said I should have them for that.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18321129-217]
218. BENJAMIN KEMP was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of November, I live tame fowl, price 2s., the property of Harry Mayo.
JOHN COOKSEY. I am in the employ of Harry Mayo, who keeps the Three Pigeons, at Brentford . I was standing in the yard, and saw the prisoner in the yard - I stopped him, and found a fowl under his jacket; its neck was broken - it had been just killed; it was alive in the morning.
HERBERT PRIDON. I am a Policeman. I received him in charge; the neck of the fowl was broken - it was warm; three more fowls were found, which had been knocked down.
Prisoner. It was not alive when I found it.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Confined Three Months, and Publicly Whipped.
[trialAccount t18321129-218]
219. WILLIAM KERRY was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of November, 1 trunk, value 2l., the goods of Joseph Upcott.
JOSEPH UPCOTT. I live in High-street, Hoxton. On the 21st of November this truck was kept by the side of the house - I had seen it there at one o'clock; I went out, and returned about half-past two - it was then gone; I saw it the next morning at No. 4, New-street, Shoe-lane Holborn; I knew it to be mine - I made it; I had seen the prisoner before - he came once to hire a chaise of me.
ANN UPCOTT. I am the prosecutor's sister. The prisoner came to me on the 24th of November, at two o'clock, to hire the truck; I saw him standing at the door, looking at it - I went and asked him his business; he said he wanted the lightest truck we had got - I told him that was the lightest we had at present; he said if I was sure it was not the lightest he was not in a hurry, and would wait an hour, till I could get him a lighter one - I said the lightest we had was out; I went to the back of the house, found a lighter one, and said I was sure there was not a lighter one than that; he said, "I suppose you want my direction?" I said Yes - he said, "Give me a bit of paper;" he gave me his direction on my brother's card: we let trucks out at 3d. an hour - I saw the truck next morning at No. 4, New-street-hill, Shoe-lane, Holborn, at Mr. Burrows' - he gave me his direction, Robert Roberts, at No. 17, Wellington-street, Kingsland-road; it has got rubbed out of the card - he was taken up the day it was found.
RICHARD PHILLIPS. I am an auctioneer. The prisoner brought a truck to me, on Wednesday, the 21st of November - he said he wished to have it sold; I asked if it belonged to him - he said it belonged to Mr. Upcott, who was just taken to Whitecross-street prison, and wished to have it sold directly, as he was in distressed circumstances - he asked for 10s. on it, which I refused, but at last let him have 5s.; I sold it for 28s., to Mr. Budden.
JOHN BUDDEN. I live in Shoe-lane. I bought the truck on the 21st, at a sale in Chiswell-street (which is not in the City), for 28s.; Upcott claimed it - it has no name on it now.
JOSEPH UPCOTT. I saw the truck at Budden's, and am certain it is mine; I never gave him authority to sell it - I was not in prison; it is all false.
FRANCIS OAKES. I am sixteen years old. I went to No. 16, Wellington-street, and inquired for the name of Roberts - he was not there.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not hire it of Mrs. Upcott; I did not know her or Mr. Upcott - I was sent to Phillips with it, and gave him the message which was given me.
GUILTY. Aged 34. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-219]
220. JOHN LEARY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of November, 1 watch, value 20s., the goods of William Pearce, from the person of Mary Pearce .
MARY PEARCE. I am the mother of William Pearce; this watch was his. On the 27th of November I had taken it out of pledge, at the corner of Fore-street, Limehouse, about one o'clock; it came on a very heavy rain, and I went into the Queen's Head public-house - I sat on a bench by the fire-side, and the prisoner was close to me; the watch was in my pocket - I had not been there ten minutes before he went away; I am quite sure the watch was in my pocket when he sat down by me, and in a minute after he left I put my hand into my pocket, and the watch was gone - he was taken the same evening; I saw him at Lambeth-street.
JOSEPH HUGHES. I am a pawnbroker. On the 27th of November the prisoner pawned this watch, for 1s., which was all he asked for it - he pawned it in the name of John Leary; the prosecutrix saw it the next day, and said it was her son's.
MARY PEARCE. The watch belonged to my son, who is in the East Indies; he had pawned it in December last year - I am certain it is the watch I redeemed.
Prisoner's Defence. It is my own - I have had it in my possession nine months; I wanted a pot of beer, and pawned it for 1s.
JAMES PEARCE. I am a son of the prosecutrix. I know my brother's watch - I know this to be the watch by the ribbon and key, and the watch; I have seen it in his possession scores of times.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-220]
221. MARGARET LACY was indicted for stealing,on the 25th of November, 2 blankets, value 3s.; 1 sheet value 1s.; 1 curtain, value 6d.; 1 frock, value 1s.; 2 petticoats, value 1s., and 1 net cap, value 6d., the goods of Phillip Arnuss - 1 gown, value 2s., and 1 pocket-book, value 6d., the goods of Alice Hurley .
ALICE HURLEY. On the 25th of November I lived at No. 1, Malborough-street - I am servant to Philip Arnuss, who keeps a public-house; the prisoner had been a servant there about seven years ago; I had lived there a fortnight; on the 25th of November, at half-past four in the evening, I made the bed in the club-room, and left the child's clothes on the bed, and the curtains were on the bed - I found the club-room door open at a quarter to seven o'clock, and found her concealed behind the door; I asked what brought her there; she said, she was a poor woman; I detained her, and called my master - I went into the bed-room, and found all the clothes off the bed; I was the only servant - nobody but her could have gone to the bed-room - the club-room is on the first floor - the articles in the indictment were gone off the bed - it was a turn-up bed which stood in the club-room.
PHILIP ARNUSS. I keep the house. The prisoner lived with me about seven years ago - Hurley called me up - I found the prisoner in the club-room - I said, "You wretch, have you come back again!" she fell on her knees, begged pardon, and said she would never come to my house any more - I sent for a Policeman - the bed was completely stripped of all the things; she had her shoes off; she has been committed twice for robbing me before - I found all these things removed from the bed, ready to be taken away - she begged me not to prosecute her.
HENRY HARRIS. I am a Policeman. I received her in charge - the things were all laying at her feet in the club-room.
Prisoner. I had not broken my fast for three days - poverty drove me to it.
GUILTY.* Aged 27. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-221]
222. JAMES LITTLETON was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of November, 1 box, value 3d., and 37 segars, value 2s., the goods of Abraham Biggs.
ABRAHAM BIGGS. I am a tobacconist, and live in Great St. Andrew-street - the prisoner was a stranger. On Saturday, the 17th of November, I went out about ten o'clock in the morning, and returned a little before one, and this box of segars was gone - I had left them safe when I went out - I found them at Bow-street, and knew them to be mine.
WILLIAM RICE. I was coming up Great St. Andrew-street on the 17th of November; I saw the prisoner and another man opposite Biggs' shop, looking in at the window; I watched them, and saw the prisoner go in - he came out in a minute, and joined the other; he had nothing then - the other then went in, came out, and went in a second time - I saw him give a box of segars to the prisoner - I ran and spoke to a Policeman - we run in pursuit of them; the prisoner turned up a gateway, and I took him with the box in his possession - it had thirty-seven segars in it; I should have had the other man taken afterwards, but could not identify his face.
JOHN STARR. I am a Policeman. Rice gave me the information - I ran and apprehended the prisoner with the segars.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I met a young man in Holborn; he asked me to go with him; we went round Seven-dials; he asked me to go into the shop and ask for Robinson - I went and told him he did not live there; he told me to wait; he then brought these things, and told me to hold them.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-222]
223. ELIZABETH MILLS was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of November, 1 horse-rug, value 6s., the goods of Thomas Whitmore.
THOMAS WHITMORE. I live at Lower-street, Islington. This rug was in my cart at the Sluice-house; the cart stood in an open shed; about four o'clock I drove home, and then missed the rug.
TIMOTHY DONOVAN. I am a Policeman. On the 19th of November, about eleven o'clock, I saw the prisoner in Aylesbury-street, in front of a public-house door - I stopped her about twelve o'clock at night, with this rug concealed about her - she said she bought it at a pawnbroker's in Aldersgate-street that evening, she could not tell me the name, nor point out the house; the prosecutor saw it next day at Hatton-garden; the marks are picked out, but the needle-holes are still there, which shows the prosecutor's initials.
JAMES ETHERIDGE. I keep a public-house in Aylesbury-street. On Monday evening, the 19th of November, a little girl and three loose characters came in; the girl said, "Let me leave this for Mrs. Mills;" I put it behind the counter; and late in the evening the prisoner came in and went out, while I was engaged; I did not see it in her possession; I have employed her at times, and would do so again.
WILLIAM MASON. I am a Policeman. I saw a young man, whom I knew very well, standing with the prisoner, and two others stood at a distance - I saw them go to the White Hart public-house, which Etheridge keeps; and just before twelve o'clock, the prisoner came up to the public-house door - the young men were in the house - they came out, crossed over, and stood there; the prisoner watched the Policeman round his beat, and when he was out of sight, she went in, and brought out the rug - I was concealed; I followed, and they were all taken; the others were discharged.
Prisoner's Defence. I go out charing - I was returning, and called to know if Eldridge wanted me, as I worked for him, but he was engaged; I came out, and three young men said, "Mrs. Mills, will you go in and fetch a bundle for us?" I went in, and before I could get to them the Policeman took me.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18321129-223]
224. JANE BRITTON was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of November, 1 cloak, value 30s., the goods of the Rev. George Peacock.
GEORGE JAMES HARRIS. I am porter at the United University club-house, Pall Mall. The Rev. George Peacock, who is tutor of Trinity-college, Cambridge, left town, and left his cloak in my care; I saw it hanging in the hall,and in about five minutes I saw the prisoner going out at the door, with it on her shoulders - she was a perfect stranger.
Cross-examined by Mr. Barry. Q. Were you the only servant in the hall? A. Yes - I was sitting in a chair in the hall, and did not see her come in; after I accused her of taking it off the peg, she said a man had thrown it on her arum outside, and she had come in to tell me of it, but I stopped her in the act of going out with it.
COURT. Q. Did she attempt to point out any man? A. No.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18321129-224]
225. WILLIAM BRIAN was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of November, 1 pocket-book, value 2s., and I bill of exchange, value 15l., the property of Charles James, from his person.
CHARLES JAMES. I am a camp-equipage maker, and live in Coventry-street. On the 30th of November, about twelve o'clock at night, I was going home, and in King-street, Covent-garden, I missed from my pocket a pocketbook, containing a bill for 15l.; I was close to the houses - a person tried to pass between me and the houses; there was not room, and during that time I felt a motion at my pocket - I looked round, and saw a person walking away from me; I put my hand to my pocket, and my book was gone - I went after the person, and the Policeman laid hold of him; it was the prisoner - I saw him drop the book while the Policeman held his collar.
JOHN JOHNSON. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner, as Mr. James said he had taken his pocket-book; both my hands were engaged - I looked down, and saw the pocket-book slip down at his feet; he seemed surprised that he should be apprehended; there were two youngsters there, who ran off, but I could not follow them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been to Chelsea, looking for employ, and then went to see my mother - I met two young men, and had a glass or two with them, and in King-street I was charged with this.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18321129-225]
226. HENRY HOLMES was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of November, 3lbs. of soap, value 18d., the goods of William Fisher.
MARY FISHER. I am the wife of William Fisher - we keep a little shop in Noble-street. On the 24th of November, about seven o'clock in the evening, the prisoner, came for a halfpenny worth of tobacco; I served him and he went out - this soap was inside the counter - I did not miss it till about half-past seven o'clock, when Mrs. Perry came to know if I had lost any; it was a whole cake - I saw the prisoner in custody with it at eleven o'clock that night - he lived next door to me.
NATHANIEL NICHOLS. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner about half-past nine o'clock at night, charged with offering the soap for sale to Mrs. Perry; Mrs. Fisher saw it at the station, and said it was impossible to swear to it, but hers was soap of that description.
CHARLOTTE PERRY. I live at St. Helena-place. On the 24th of October a little boy came to sell me this soap, and asked 1s. for it - I asked where he got it; he said his father sent him out to sell it - I said I suspected it was stolen, and should detain it till his father came; I went to the door after him, - the prisoner came up to the door, and said, "Give me that soap, Mrs. Perry - it is mine;" I refused, and went round to the neighbours, to inquire if they had lost any; Fisher claimed it.
JOHN STANLEY. I am thirteen years old, and have known the prisoner three years. On the 24th of November, about a quarter-past seven o'clock, I saw him in Noble-street - he gave me this soap, and told me to go and sell it at Perry's, and say my father sent me with it; I went to her with it - I am sure I have told the truth.
MARY FISHER. I lost the same kind of soap as this; the prisoner's parents are unable to support him, but can send him to sea now.
GUILTY. Aged 13. - Judgment Respited .

230. MARY BARDIN was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of November, 4 silver spoons, value 12s., the goods of William Shepherd.
MARY SHEPHERD. I am the wife of William Shepherd. I have known the prisoner from a child; she visited me on the 15th and 16th of November, and drank tea on each occasion, and on the morning of the 17th I missed four silver spoons.
THOMAS FRUGIE (Police-constable D 161). I apprehended the prisoner in Chapel-street; I told her what I took her for - she made on answer; the prosecutor said to her at the watch-house, "Mary, have you sold my spoons?" she said No, but she had pawned them in Skinner-street -I found two of them.
WILLIAM ALFRED QUTNAM. I am shopman to a pawnbroker. I have two spoons, which were pawned by the prisoner on the 17th of November, for 4s.

231. WILLIAM TATE was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of November, 1 bottle-jack, value 5s.; 2 salt-cellars, value 2s.; 1 iron fork, value 11s., and 11 iron skewers, value 1s., the goods of the Directors of the Guardians of the Poor of the Parish of St. Marylebone .
The prisoner being deaf and dumb had the evidence communicated to him by signs.
MR. BARRY conducted the prosecution.
ISAAC TWILLEY. I am master of the workhouse of St. Marylebone. I have known the prisoner some years - he was in the workhouse; on the 23rd of November I left him in the store-room to clean it out, and on the following day he obtained an order to go out improperly - he was brought back some time afterwards, and these articles, which were missing from the store-room, found on him.
AMBROSE PERRY (Police-constable Q 20.) I took the prisoner on the evening of the 24th of November, in Hyde-park - I found these articles on him.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
