232. GEORGE CRAVEN was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of November, 1 box, value 6d., and 56lbs. of raisins, value 28s., the goods of William Perry.
STEPHEN HOBBS. I am in the service of Mr. William Perry, a grocer. On the evening of the 29th of November I was in the counting-house, and a person called to me; I went out, but did not see the prisoner till he was brought back.
JOHN BRUNDLEY. I am an inspector of Police. I heard a cry of Stop thief! in Wellington-street - the prisoner was pointed out to me; I took him, and brought him back - Mr. Hobbs has the box of raisins, which I understood the prisoner dropped in the street; I told him what he was charged with; he said he did not steal it, but was employed by a respectable man to take it.
SARAH MARCH. I saw the prisoner drop the box off his shoulder, nearly opposite the prosecutor's shop; the shopman took it up, in my presence - I am sure he is the man.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A respectable man met me, and asked me to carry the box for him; I carried it a few yards, and he told me to go on the opposite side of the way - a man then stopped me, and asked where I got it; I said, "From that man," and pointed to him - the man ran after him, and so did I, but they could not catch him.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321129-232]
233. ANN COOPER was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of October, 1 sovereign, 1 half-sovereign, and 2 half-crowns, the monies of Thomas Henman, from his person.
THOMAS HENMAN. I am an attorney, and live in Brydges-street, Covent-garden. On the 27th of October, between four and six o'clock in the morning, I met a woman, who gave her as name Smith; I accompanied her to a house in Eagle-court - I was not quite sober, but knew what I was about; we went into a room on the ground floor, on the left of the passage - the prisoner came into the room in a minute after we got there; I had pulled off my coat and waistcoat, but that was all I had done - there was no bed in that room, but a sort of couch; soon after the prisoner came in, a man came in, and it was proposed to have some gin, by the woman I went with; I gave a shilling to send for some - a woman named Martin fetched it; when the gin came, Martin who appeared to be the servant, poured out a glass, and handed it to me; I said, "Hand it that way," meaning to the man, who was then standing by the fire-place -Martin appeared to be doing so; the prisoner said,"Don't be a b - y fool: don't you know the greatest stranger always drinks first?" the glass was then handed to me - I was simple enough to drink the contents; I left some dregs - what I drank produced a great effect upon me, and while I was coughing, I saw the man take the glass from me, and rince it out twice - I saw that distinctly; the gin was then handed round, and finished - I found my power of motion gone; I could not rise from my seat, for fear of choaking myself; I never felt so before, I sat quite immoveable; Cooper and Martin took me by my arm into the next room, on the same floor, and Smith came in there; I began to recover - Smith was sleeping: I aroused her, and said, "You d - d thief, you have robbed me;" I put my hand into my pocket when I went into the house, and saw my money safe - I had a sovereign, a half-sovereign, three half-crowns, and a shilling, and soon after we had had the gin, I saw the prisoner go to my coat and waistcoat, which were in a chair opposite to where I sat, and saw her take my money out of my pocket; the man said to her, "Don't be a b - y fool, leave him some browus," and the prisoner returned some back - when I told Smith of this in the next room, she said, so help her God! she had seen nothing wrong; I went into the passage, and saw the prisoner - I said to her, "You are the woman, who robbed me;" she denied it - then she and the man caught hold of me by the arms; I struggled, and got from them to the door - I called the Policeman, who came and took the prisoner; the man got away - there is a passage right through the house; I have never got my money - the prisoner said in going to the station, that the money was under the chair - we went, but could not find it; she offered 5l. not to be taken.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. You are a solicitor? A. Yes, I was clerk to Silvester and Walter, but left them in August - I am on the roll of attorneys; it might be about five o'clock in the morning when this happened - I have two or three times a week to get up at half-past two o'clock; I had had a glass of ale, and a glass of gin and water at the Yorkshire Gray, in Brydges-street, and it was there I met Smith - I had 2l. 2s. in my pocket when I went out, and a few halfpence; I was robbed of 1l. 15s. - I had 3s. 6d. left in my pocket and some halfpence; I am not in the habit of going to houses of this sort - I did not object to the man being in the room; I did not say "I will have two any how," and then catch hold of Martin - I did not after I lost my money go into the room for half an hour with Smith.
WILLIAM COOPER (Police-constable F 82). I heard the cry, and found the prosecutor at the house - he charged the persons there with stealing from him a sovereign and a half, and two half-crowns; he had his senses, and described all the circumstances as he has now - I found the prisoner Martin and Smith there; I took them all, and went in search of the man, but could not find him - there was a passage through the house, so that a person could go through; Cooper denied the charge, but in going to the station she said if we would go back we should find the money under the chair, but it has not been found - she offered the prosecutor 5l. to let her go, and offered me the same; the other two were remanded three times and then discharged.
Prisoner's Defence. I said I would sooner have given 5l. than such a thing should have happened - when the prosecutor went into the back parlour, Smith said to him,"Will you come into the bed-room, for the sofa is not fit to sit down on;" when they were in the bed-room there was some quarrel and Smith called me - I went to the door; I found it locked - Smith said, "This man is quarrelling with me and using me very ill;" I said, "I am sure you should not, if you are tipsy you should not ill use her;" she then took his coat and waistcoat from the nail - the prosecutor and her stopped for the money which fell down; the prosecutor said, "It is my money."
THOMAS HENMAN. I did not pick any money up, nor did I state so - my coat and waistcoat were handed to me by Martin and I threw them on my arm.
GUILTY (of stealing only.) Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-233]
234. EVAN CAPE was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of November, 1 cloth cap, value 2s., the goods of Charles Oswin, from the person of Charles Holley Oswin .
CHARLES HOLLEY OSWIN. I am the son of Charles Oswin - he lives in Harley-street, and is a dentist. On the 24th of November, I was in Vere-street, taking a letter to the Post, and the prisoner passed me - he took my cap, and ran on to Old Cavendish-street; there he threw it down, and I took it up - the officer followed, and took him in Oxford-street.
GEORGE MILLS (Police-constable D 84.) I saw the prisoner running - Oswin charged him with taking the cap- I followed him to Oxford-street, and took him.

235. JOHN DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of November, 1 Venetian blind, value 12s., the goods of Joseph Lavender.
JAMES BRADFIELD. I live with Mr. Lavender, a blind-maker, in Oxford-street. I placed this blind in his window half an hour before it was taken.
HENRY BAKER. I am an officer. About eight o'clock in the evening of the 12th of November, I saw the prisoner and another loitering about the shop in Oxford-street - the other went in, and came out again; the prisoner then took the blind, and came out.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a person come out of the shop with this blind - he ran, and placed it six doors off against a door-post; a woman said, "I think that man has been stealing something;" I went upto him - the Policeman came, and took me; I never touched the blind, and was not near it - I said, "You are mistaken;" he said," It does not matter, you will do quite as well," and then the woman who saw it followed us, making a noise, and kept saying, "That is not the man;" the officer threatened that if she followed him he would take her, or I should have had a witness here.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Three Months.

236. GEORGE FORD was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of November, 2 tea-trays, value 1l. 7s.; 1 breadtray, value 3s.; 1 pair of snuffers and tray, value 1s. 6d.; 1 pair of candlesticks, value 4s.; 2 curtain-bands, value 4s.; 5 brass finger-plates, value 4s.; 2 brass knobs, value 1s.; 3 brass hooks, value 6d.; 1 milk-pot, value 2s. 6d.; 1 sugar-basin, value 3s. 6d.; 1 salt, value 1s. 6d.; 2 egg cups, value 9d.; 5 apoons, value 10d.; 2 tin pans, value 2s.; 1 brush, value 2s., and 1 hasp and staple, value 3d., the goods of Joseph James Sheffield.
JOSEPH SHEFFIELD JAMES. I am an ironmonger. The prisoner had been my errand-boy for six or seven years; in consequence of the loss of some pigeons, I went to his father's residence on the 15th of November - he sent a young woman on Thursday morning to say he was not able to attend to his duty - I found at his father's, the whole of the property stated in the indictment, some of which has my private mark on it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOSEPH BEAL (Police-constable 252 K.) I found the property at the prisoner's father's, where I am informed he resides.
WILLIAM GEORGE SMITH. I am a biscuit-baker. I am an acquaintance of the prosecutor's, and knew the prisoner lived with him - I met him on the 28th of November, in the Minories - he saw me, and turned down John-street; I followed him through several streets, when he turned the corner, he ran, as I could perceive, by the distance he got- I at last came up to him, and said it was useless his trying to run away, as I should give him in charge - he said he would go with me; I delivered him to the officer; I said he had acted very foolishly in going on as he had - he cried, and said the only thing he had taken was a seal.
JAMES JOHN CONNERLY (Police-constable 135 H). The prisoner was given into my charge by Mr. Smith; I made him no promise or threat, but said, "You are a very foolish young man;" he said he had taken a tea-board or two, and some other articles, which Mr. Sheffield valued at 4l., but he had got them back again.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Will you swear you did not say it would be better for him to tell you? A. No. I did not.
GEORGE AUGUSTUS. I am a house-agent. I know the house the property was found in; I let it to the prisoner, and he paid the rent of the premises.
Cross-examined. Q. He had a good character? A. I believe he has.
GUILTY. - Aged 22. Confined for Three Months.

237. CHARLES JOHN FOX was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of November, 1 set of bed-furniture, value 2l. 5s.; 1 counterpane, value 10s.; 3 pillowcases, value 4s., and 1 towel, value 1s., the goods of John Thomas; and that he had before been convicted of felony.
JOHN THOMAS. I live in Compton-street, Chelsea; the prisoner lodged a fortnight with me. I had a large clothes-chest there, which was broken open, and a set of bed-furniture, a counterpane, and the other articles stated, taken from it; I was informed he had gone out, and took him in a public-house, offering the counterpane for sale.
JOHN BREEZE. My sister and I keep a public-house. On the 17th of November the prisoner came in, and offered some pillow-cases and a napkin to me, and to others who were in the tap-room; the prosecutor came in - he was then tying up his things.
JOHN HIGGS. The prisoner left a bundle of things at my house, between seven and eight o'clock. I keep the Pitt's Head, the corner of Old-street.(Property produced and sworn to)
JOHN ROBINSON (Police-constable G 57). I produce a certificate of the former conviction of the prisoner; I was a witness against him, and know he is the man.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY.* Aged 58. - Transported for Fourteen Years.

SAMUEL MARCH. 239. MARGARET HARTY was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of October, 6 bonnets, value 11s.; 6 picture-frames, value 7s.; 6 pictures, value 16s., and 1 dress, value 15s., the goods of Edward Russell; and that she had been before convicted of felony.

SAMUEL RUSSELL. These three bonnets are mine; I lost six, and six picture-frames and a dress - these are all that are found.
JAMES WARD (Police-constable C 172). I produce a certificate of the prisoner's former conviction - I was present, and know she is the person.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing at all about the things; my husband took the room.

240. WILLIAM HUNT was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of November, 1 sovereign, the money of Thomas Lee.
WILLIAM FORD. I am errand-boy to Thomas Lee, of Collingwood-street. I went to Mr. Harris', and ordered half a hundred of coals; the prisoner, who is his errand-boy, brought them to my mistress' door - my mistress gave me a sovereign to pay for them; when I got to the door - he told me to toss the sovereign up, and see how bright it would look; I tossed it up, and it fell on the stones - I ran, and got a light; I left him there the while, and then we got a birch broom, and all looked for it, but we never found it.
ELIZA LEE. I gave this boy the sovereign - he came and said he had dropped it; I found the prisoner at the door, looking for it, but we could not find it - I have never seen it since; the prisoner said it did not roll.
THOMAS LEE. The sovereigns was mine - it was lost on Saturday night; I saw the prisoner when it was lost; I sent for him the following morning, and he had a pair of new boots on - I gave him into custody.
RICHARD HUNT (Police-constable H 70). I had information, and went to the prisoner's mother on Sunday evening, and found her very much in liquor, laying on the floor - the prisoner was not at home; I asked her where he was, but she was too drunk to tell me - I went out, saw him in the street, and took him into custody; I asked him if he had had the sovereign - he said No; I asked him again, and he said Yes, he had - I asked what he had done with it; he pointed to a bird and cage, and said that was part of it.
JAMES JOHN CONNELLY (Police-constable H 135). I have the bird and cage, cap, and boots; the inspector was booking the charge, and asked the prisoner what he had done with it - he said he had had the sovereign, and bought a pair of new boots, a cap, and some fire-works; he gave his mother 1s. 6d., and his father 4s; I was sent to the house, and found his father and mother both intoxicated; I brought the bird and cage, fire-works, and cap down to the office - his father and mother were discharged.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-240]
241. CHARLES CLARK and ANN CLARK were indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 1 featherbed, value 15s.; 1 bolster, value 5s.; 2 pillows, value 2s.; 2 blankets, value 5s.; 1 quilt, value 2s., and 3 pictures, value 2s., the goods of William Bird.
MARGARET BIRD. I am the wife of William Bird - we live in Regent-place. The prisoners lodged in my house for six weeks; they had only one room - they livedtogether as man and wife; they told the Magistrate they were not married - the woman was generally locked in her room, and very seldom had her door open; she was beating her child - I went up to see what she was beating it for, and missed the property.
WILLIAM HENRY LLOYD. I am a pawnbroker. I have a quilt, which was pawned for 1s., by an elderly female. not the prisoner.
HENRY GILBERT WELLS (Police-constable B 138.) I apprehended the two prisoners - the woman gave me these duplicates out of a little bag; they led me to the pawnbroker's - the man was not there, but came home afterwards.
JAMES PANTON. I am a pawnbroker. I have three pillows, one bolster, and one blanket, which were pawned most of them, to the best of my rcollection, by the female prisoner; none of them by the man.
JOHN REYNOLDS. I have a blanket, pawned by the female prisoner.
HORACE PECKOVER. I have two pictures, pawned by the female prisoner.
MARGARET BIRD. These are my property.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I believe the man was in the habit of going out early in the morning to work, and coming home late at night? A. Yes, he was.
Ann Clark. It is perfectly clear it was my own doing.
A. CLARK - GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
C. CLARK - NOT GUILTY.

242. WILLIAM HILL was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of November, 13 padlocks, value 3s.; 2 bedwinches, value 1s.; 3 pairs of snuffers, value 1s.; 5 corkscrews, value 1s. 6d., and 2 snuffer-trays, value 4d., the goods of James Connolly.
JAMES CONNOLLY. On Thursday, the 8th of November, I went into the George and Crown public-house, Broad-street, with these goods, consisting of some locks and other articles - I put them down by my left-hand side, and turned round to show the goods I had to sell to some other persons, and this pocket was gone; I do not know who took it till the Saturday following.
JOHN STORR (Police-constable F 78). I stopped the prisoner with this pack on the Thursday night - I asked what he had got; he said he did not know, but a cab-man gave it to him to carry; there were thirteen padlocks, some cork-screws, and other things - a key was found in his pocket, which fitted one of the locks.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I found them.

[trialAccount t18321129-242]
243. WILLIAM JOHNSON and WILLIAM CROSS were indicted for stealing, on the 24th of November, 45 yards of flannel, value 2l., the goods of James Chapple.
WILLIAM BRISTY. I live with Mr. James Chapple, of High-street, Pentonville. On the 24th of November I was behind the counter - Johnson came to the door, took up the flannel, and ran away with it: I ran out, and saw him carrying the flannel, with Cross behind him, not one door off - they were taken about two doors off the house; this is the flannel - I marked it on Friday night, and it was taken on Saturday night.
JAMES FOX (Police-constable N 196). I was on duty near this shop on Saturday night, the 24th of November - I saw the prisoner go to the door together, and which took the flannel I could not tell, but Johnson had it when I took them into custody.
JOHNSON - GUILTY. Aged 24.
CROSS - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18321129-243]
244. ANN JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 1 canvas bag, value 2d., and 8 sovereigns, the property of William Incell, from his person.
WILLIAM INCELL. I am a corn-chandler, and live on Stepney-green. On the 26th of November, at twenty minutes after nine o'clock in the evening, I met the prisoner in Whitechapel-road - she was a stranger; she asked where I was going - I said to Stepney; she said she lived there herself, and we walked on together - it was rather dark at the time; I had been taking a glass with a few friends - all on a sudden, when we got to Hanibal-street, she said she saw somebody she knew; I felt my purse, which contained eight sovereigns, drawn from my trouser's pocket - we were stopping at the time, as she said she lived there; I called the Policeman, who came and took her - I had not given her the purse, nor taken any liberty with her.
GEORGE WALTER SHEW (Police-constable K 50). I was called, and saw the prosecutor holding the prisoner - she wanted to get away; it was about half-past eight o'clock - he accused her of stealing his money, and she denied it; my brother-officer searched her - I heard a sovereign drop, and picked it up; I found the purse after she had gone to the station, about two yards from where I first saw her - it was empty; seven sovereigns were found tied in her shawl.
WILLIAM HOMER (Police-constable K 173). I took off the prisoner's shawl at the station, to search her, but the inspector said he would send for a woman - the shawl was then on the table - the inspector took it up, and found seven sovereigns, tied up at the end of it; this is the money.
Prisoner's Defence. He accosted me first; the sovereigns are not his - he offered me 1s.; the purse fell down, and I took it up.
GUILTY. Aged 30. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-244]
245. THOMAS JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of November, 1 pair of trousers, value 4s. 6d., the goods of Samuel Dias.
SAMUEL DIAS. I live in Brick-lane. These trousers were hanging on a pole, tied round with a rope - I did not see them taken, but my attention was drawn by my servant - the prisoner was brought back after I gave information.
FRANCES FLOOD. I live with the prosecutor - I cannot swear to the prisoner, but I saw a hand go up, and the trousers pulled down - I gave information, and saw the prisoner brought back to the house afterwards.
LEWIS HARRIS. The prosecutor on Wednesday, the 28th, called at my master's, and said he had lost a pair of trousers - the prisoner afterwards came and offered the trousers for sale, and I gave information.
THOMAS FENTON. I was sent by Harris to the prosecutor, with the prisoner and the trousers.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. My brother came home and asked me to go to buy him a waistcoat - I was going down Petticoat-lane, and saw a man with these trousers; he asked 6s. for them, and taking some of my brother's money, I went to pawn them, to get the money back.
GUILTY.* Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321129-245]
246. GEORGE JORDAN was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of October, 4lbs. of lamb, value 3s., the goods of John Williams.
JOHN WILLIAMS. I had this lamb in my hand at the corner of Milton-street; I turned to speak to two girls, and the prisoner snatched it from me - I pursued him, but I fell down; he got off, and I did not see him again till he was in custody - I knew him very well.
PAUL PLANT (Police-constable G 156). I took the prisoner for smashing, and as I thought he resembled the person who took the lamb, I took the prosecutor to him.
Prisoner's Defence. He was very much in liquor - two women had told of his arm; he asked me to have something to drink - we went into the Paul - I staid there some time, and then he said I had got his shoulder of mutton; I said I had not - he said, "Has Jenny got it;" I said, "I don't know;" we then came out - I was afterwards taken on another charge; I saw no mutton with him.
JOHN WILLIAMS. I had not drank with him, nor had any intimacy with him; I was sober - I changed a sovereign at the Paul, and lost 9s. 6d. there.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321129-246]
247. CHARLOTTE VINCENT was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of November, 1 shirt, value 4s., the goods of Robert Farrow.
ELIZABETH FARROW. I am a laundress, and am the wife of Robert Farrow - he lives in Luton-street. This linen was brought into the house for me to wash, and in about three minutes it was gone; I went to the station-house, and then to some pawnbrokers, and found the shirt - I did not see the prisoner; my child described the prisoner to me - the things were taken from the parlour, the door of which was open.
HENRY SWAIN. I am a pawnbroker - I live about a quarter of a mile from the prosecutor's I have a shirt which was pawned by the prisoner, on the 24th of November, about nine o'clock at night - this is it.
WILLIAM JOHNS (Police-constable D 41.) The prosecutrix came to me, said she had lost a shirt, and gave me a description of the girl and her dress - I went to Little Church-street, and found the prisoner at a temporary playhouse.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18321129-247]
248. SARAH ROBINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of November, 1 watch, value 50s., the goods of Robert Lumley, from his person.
The prosecutor did not appear NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321129-248]
249. ROBERT HERN was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of November, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Peter Dunne, from his person.
PETER DUNNE. On the 27th of November, about ten o'clock in the morning, I was in Compton-street, Soho - a witness said my pocket had been picked; I then felt, and missed my handkerchief, which was safe five or ten minutes before, when I left my employer's house; I have not found it.
GEORGE ISRAEL. I am a jeweller. I saw the prisoner lift up the tail of the prosecutor's coat, and draw his handchief out; he was with three others - he was about to put it in his bosom; I lost sight of them for half a minute, then went and informed the prosecutor - we came up to the prisoner at the corner of Walker's-court; he made great resistance - I am sure he is the one who took it; he was the shortest of them.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you know him before? A. No.
THOMAS WILSON. I am a constable. I saw the prisoner making a desperate resistance - I came up and took him.
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw the handkerchief.
NOT GUILTY
